[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No assessment for penalty

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** No assessment shall be
made for any penalty imposed under section 254.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period for assessments

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) to (7),
  no assessment shall be made for any duty, interest or other amount
  payable under this Act

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an assessment of the duty payable
    for a reporting period, more than four years after the later of the
    day on which the return for the period was required to be filed and
    the day on which the return was filed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an assessment for any other amount
    payable under this Act, more than four years after the amount became
    payable; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an assessment for an amount for
    which a trustee in bankruptcy became liable under section 212, after
    the earlier of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the day that is 90 days after the return on which
      the assessment is based is filed with, or other evidence of the
      facts on which the assessment is based comes to the attention of,
      the Minister, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the expiry of the period referred to in
      paragraph (a) or (b), whichever applies in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation re refund assessment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) to (7), an assessment of
  the amount of a refund or any other payment that may be obtained under
  this Act may be made at any time but a reassessment or additional
  assessment of an amount paid or applied as a refund under this Act or
  of an amount paid as interest in respect of an amount paid or applied
  as a refund under this Act shall not be made more than four years
  after the application for the amount was filed in accordance with this
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) do not apply to a
  reassessment of a person made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to give effect to a decision on an objection or
    appeal;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the consent in writing of the person to
    dispose of an appeal; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to give effect to an alternative basis or argument
    advanced by the Minister under subsection (7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No limitation on assessment if fraud,
  etc.

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An assessment in respect of any matter may be made at
  any time if the person to be assessed has, in respect of that matter,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} made a misrepresentation that is attributable to
    their neglect, carelessness or wilful default;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} committed fraud

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in making or filing a return under this Act,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in making or filing an application for a refund
      under this Act, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in supplying or failing to supply any
      information under this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} filed a waiver under subsection (8) that is in
    effect at that time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No limitation if payment for another
  reporting period

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, in making an assessment, the Minister determines
  that a person has paid in respect of any matter an amount as or on
  account of duty payable for a reporting period of the person that was
  payable for another reporting period of the person, the Minister may
  at any time make an assessment for that other period in respect of
  that matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction of duty --- reporting period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the result of a reassessment on an objection to,
  or a decision on an appeal from, an assessment is to reduce the amount
  of duty payable by a person and, by reason of the reduction, any
  refund or other payment claimed by the person for a reporting period,
  or in an application for a refund or other payment, should be
  decreased, the Minister may at any time assess or reassess that
  reporting period or that application only for the purpose of taking
  the reduction of duty into account in respect of the refund or other
  payment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternative basis or argument

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may advance an alternative basis or
  argument in support of an assessment of a person, or in support of all
  or any portion of the total amount determined on assessment to be
  payable or remittable by a person under this Act, at any time after
  the period otherwise limited by subsection (1) or (2) for making the
  assessment unless on an appeal under this Act

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there is relevant evidence that the person is no
    longer able to adduce without the leave of the court; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is not appropriate in the circumstances for the
    court to order that the evidence be adduced.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} If a reassessment of a person is made that gives
  effect to an alternative basis or argument advanced by the Minister
  under subsection (7) in support of a particular assessment of the
  person, the Minister shall not reassess for an amount that is greater
  than the total amount of the particular assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(7.2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (7.1) does not apply to any portion of
  an amount determined on reassessment that the Minister would be
  entitled to reassess under this Act at any time after the period
  otherwise limited by subsection (1) or (2) for making the reassessment
  if this Act were read without reference to subsection (7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Any person may, within the time otherwise limited by
  subsection (1) or (2) for an assessment, waive the application of
  subsection (1) or (2) by filing with the Minister a waiver in the
  prescribed form and manner specifying the matter in respect of which
  the person waives the application of that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revoking waiver

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Any person who files a waiver under subsection (8)
  may revoke it on six months notice to the Minister by filing with the
  Minister a notice of revocation of the waiver in the prescribed form
  and manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 191
- 2010, c. 25, s. 117
- 2016, c. 12, s. 100
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191-20101215.html "Link to previous version of section 191"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister not bound

- **[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister is not bound by any return, application or information
  provided by or on behalf of a person and may make an assessment
  despite any return, application or information provided or not
  provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability not affected

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Liability to pay any duty, interest or other amount
  is not affected by an incorrect or incomplete assessment or by the
  fact that no assessment has been made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding effect --- unincorporated body

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a person (referred to in this subsection as the
  "body") that is not an individual or a corporation is assessed in
  respect of any matter,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the assessment is not invalid only because one or
    more other persons (each of which is referred to in this subsection
    as a "representative") who are liable for obligations of the body
    did not receive a notice of the assessment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the assessment is binding on each representative of
    the body, subject to a reassessment of the body and the rights of
    the body to object to or appeal from the assessment under this Act;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an assessment of a representative in respect of the
    same matter is binding on the representative, subject only to a
    reassessment of the representative and the rights of the
    representative to object to or appeal from the assessment of the
    representative under this Act on the grounds that the representative
    is not a person who is liable to pay an amount to which the
    assessment of the body relates, the body has been reassessed in
    respect of that matter or the assessment of the body in respect of
    that matter has been vacated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment deemed valid

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to being reassessed or vacated as a result of
  an objection or appeal under this Act, an assessment is deemed to be
  valid and binding despite any error, defect or omission in the
  assessment or in any proceeding under this Act relating to it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Irregularities

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An appeal from an assessment shall not be allowed by
  reason only of an irregularity, informality, omission or error on the
  part of any person in the observation of a directory provision of this
  Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of assessment

- **[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After making an assessment, the
  Minister shall send to the person assessed a notice of the assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of assessment may include assessments in
  respect of any number or combination of reporting periods, refunds or
  amounts payable under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 193
- 2010, c. 25, s. 118
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment payable

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** Any amount assessed by
the Minister is payable by the person assessed as of the date of the
assessment.

### [Objections to Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-181095 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection to assessment

- **[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person who has been assessed and who objects to the assessment may,
  within 90 days after the date of the notice of the assessment, file
  with the Minister a notice of objection in the prescribed form and
  manner setting out the reasons for the objection and all relevant
  facts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issue to be decided

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice of objection shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reasonably describe each issue to be decided;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify in respect of each issue the relief sought,
    expressed as the change in any amount that is relevant for the
    purposes of the assessment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide the facts and reasons relied on by the
    person in respect of each issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Late compliance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), if a notice of objection does
  not include the information required under paragraph (2)(b) or (c) in
  respect of an issue to be decided that is described in the notice, the
  Minister may, in writing, request the person to provide the
  information, and that paragraph is deemed to be complied with in
  respect of the issue if, within 60 days after the request is made, the
  person submits the information in writing to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on objections

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if a person has filed a
  notice of objection to an assessment (in this subsection referred to
  as the "earlier assessment") and the Minister makes a particular
  assessment under subsection (8) as a result of the notice of
  objection, unless the earlier assessment was made in accordance with
  an order of a court vacating, varying or restoring an assessment or
  referring an assessment back to the Minister for reconsideration and
  reassessment, the person may object to the particular assessment in
  respect of an issue

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} only if the person complied with subsection (2) in
    the notice with respect to that issue; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} only with respect to the relief sought in respect
    of that issue as specified by the person in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsection (4)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If a person has filed a notice of objection to an
  assessment (in this subsection referred to as the "earlier
  assessment") and the Minister makes a particular assessment under
  subsection (8) as a result of the notice of objection, subsection (4)
  does not limit the right of the person to object to the particular
  assessment in respect of an issue that was part of the particular
  assessment and not part of the earlier assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on objections

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), no objection may be made by a
  person in respect of an issue for which the right of objection has
  been waived in writing by the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance of objection

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may accept a notice of objection even
  though it was not filed in the prescribed form and manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of objection

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a notice of objection, the Minister
  shall, without delay, reconsider the assessment and vacate or confirm
  it or make a reassessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiving reconsideration

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} If, in a notice of objection, a person who wishes to
  appeal directly to the Tax Court requests the Minister not to
  reconsider the assessment objected to, the Minister may confirm the
  assessment without reconsideration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of decision

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} After reconsidering an assessment under
  subsection (8) or confirming an assessment under subsection (9), the
  Minister shall send notice of the Minister's decision by registered or
  certified mail to the person objecting.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time by Minister

- **[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  no objection to an assessment is filed under section 195 within the
  time limited under this Act, a person may make an application to the
  Minister to extend the time for filing a notice of objection and the
  Minister may grant the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must set out the reasons why the
  notice of objection was not filed within the time limited under this
  Act for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made by delivering or mailing
  to the Chief of Appeals in a Tax Services Office or Taxation Centre of
  the Agency the application accompanied by a copy of the notice of
  objection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defect in application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may accept an application even though it
  was not made in accordance with subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of Minister

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of an application, the Minister shall,
  without delay, consider the application and grant or refuse it, and
  shall notify the person of the decision by registered or certified
  mail.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Date of objection if application
  granted

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If an application is granted, the notice of objection
  is deemed to have been filed on the day of the decision of the
  Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions --- grant of application

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No application shall be granted under this section
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made within one year after the
    expiry of the time limited under this Act for objecting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time limited under this Act for
      objecting, the person

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to give a mandate to act
        in their name, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
        intention to object to the assessment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} given the reasons set out in the application and
      the circumstances of the case, it would be just and equitable to
      grant the application, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as
      circumstances permitted it to be made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 196
- 2007, c. 18, s. 113
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 196"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Appeal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-181146 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time by Tax Court

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  person who has made an application under section 196 may apply to the
  Tax Court to have the application granted after either

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister has refused the application; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 90 days have elapsed after the application was made
    and the Minister has not notified the person of the Minister's
    decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application may not be made

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made after the expiry of 30
  days after the decision referred to in subsection 196(5) was mailed to
  the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made by filing in the Registry
  of the Tax Court, in accordance with the [Tax Court of Canada
  Act](/eng/acts/T-2), three copies of the documents delivered or mailed
  under subsection 196(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to the Commissioner

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court must send a copy of the application to
  the Commissioner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Court

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court may dispose of an application by
  dismissing or granting it and, in granting it, the Court may impose
  any terms that it considers just or order that the notice of objection
  be deemed to be a valid objection as of the date of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application to be granted

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No application shall be granted under this section
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application under subsection 196(1) was made
    within one year after the expiry of the time limited under this Act
    for objecting; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time limited under this Act for
      objecting, the person

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to give a mandate to act
        in their name, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
        intention to object to the assessment,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} given the reasons set out in the application
      under this section and the circumstances of the case, it would be
      just and equitable to grant the application, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application under subsection 196(1) was
      made as soon as circumstances permitted it to be made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 197
- 2007, c. 18, s. 114
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-197-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 197"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Tax Court

- **[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person
  who has filed a notice of objection to an assessment may appeal to the
  Tax Court to have the assessment vacated or a reassessment made after

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister has confirmed the assessment or has
    reassessed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 180 days have elapsed after the filing of the
    notice of objection and the Minister has not notified the person
    that the Minister has vacated or confirmed the assessment or has
    reassessed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No appeal under subsection (1) may be instituted
  after the expiry of 90 days after notice that the Minister has
  reassessed or confirmed the assessment is sent to the person under
  subsection 195(10).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court may, on any terms that it sees fit,
  authorize a person who has instituted an appeal in respect of a matter
  to amend the appeal to include any further assessment in respect of
  the matter that the person is entitled under this section to appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time to appeal

- **[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  no appeal to the Tax Court under section 198 has been instituted
  within the time limited by that section for doing so, a person may
  make an application to the Tax Court for an order extending the time
  within which an appeal may be instituted, and the Court may make an
  order extending the time for appealing and may impose any terms that
  it considers just.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must set out the reasons why the
  appeal was not instituted within the time limited under section 198
  for doing so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}How application made

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application must be made by filing in the Registry
  of the Tax Court, in accordance with the [Tax Court of Canada
  Act](/eng/acts/T-2), three copies of the application together with
  three copies of the notice of appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy to Deputy Attorney General of
  Canada

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Tax Court must send a copy of the application to
  the office of the Deputy Attorney General of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When order to be made

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No order shall be made under this section unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the application is made within one year after the
    expiry of the time limited under section 198 for appealing; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person demonstrates that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} within the time limited under section 198 for
      appealing, the person

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} was unable to act or to give a mandate to act
        in their name, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} had a [bona fide]{.otherLang lang="la"}
        intention to appeal,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} given the reasons set out in the application and
      the circumstances of the case, it would be just and equitable to
      grant the application,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the application was made as soon as
      circumstances permitted it to be made, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} there are reasonable grounds for appealing from
      the assessment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 199
- 2007, c. 18, s. 115
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-199-20030101.html "Link to previous version of section 199"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on appeals to the Tax Court

- **[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 198, if a person has
  filed a notice of objection to an assessment, the person may appeal to
  the Tax Court to have the assessment vacated, or a reassessment made,
  only with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an issue in respect of which the person has
    complied with subsection 195(2) in the notice and only with respect
    to the relief sought in respect of the issue as specified by the
    person in the notice; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an issue described in subsection 195(5) if the
    person was not required to file a notice of objection to the
    assessment that gave rise to the issue.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No appeal if waiver

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 198, a person may not appeal to the
  Tax Court to have an assessment vacated or varied in respect of an
  issue for which the right of objection or appeal has been waived in
  writing by the person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Institution of appeals

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** An appeal to the Tax
Court under this Act shall be instituted in accordance with the [Tax
Court of Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to the Commissioner

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** If an appeal is made to
the Tax Court under section 18.3001 of the [Tax Court of Canada
Act](/eng/acts/T-2), the Court shall immediately send a copy of the
notice of appeal to the office of the Commissioner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** The Tax Court may
dispose of an appeal from an assessment by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} dismissing it; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} allowing it and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} vacating the assessment, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} referring the assessment back to the Minister for
    reconsideration and reassessment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to Tax Court

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister and another person agree in writing that a question
  arising under this Act, in respect of any assessment or proposed
  assessment, should be determined by the Tax Court, that question shall
  be determined by that Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time during consideration not to count

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of making an assessment of a person
  who agreed in writing to the determination of a question, filing a
  notice of objection to an assessment or instituting an appeal from an
  assessment, the time between the day on which proceedings are
  instituted in the Tax Court to have a question determined and the day
  on which the question is finally determined shall not be counted in
  the computation of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the four-year periods referred to in section 191;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which a notice of objection to an
    assessment may be filed under section 195; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period within which an appeal may be instituted
    under section 198.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference of common questions to Tax
Court

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister is of the opinion that a question arising out of one and
  the same transaction or occurrence or series of transactions or
  occurrences is common to assessments or proposed assessments in
  respect of two or more persons, the Minister may apply to the Tax
  Court for a determination of the question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application shall set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the question in respect of which the Minister
    requests a determination;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the names of the persons that the Minister seeks to
    have bound by the determination of the question; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the facts and reasons on which the Minister relies
    and on which the Minister based or intends to base assessments of
    each person named in the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the application shall be served by the
  Minister on each of the persons named in it and on any other person
  who, in the opinion of the Tax Court, is likely to be affected by the
  determination of the question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination by Tax Court of question

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Tax Court is satisfied that a determination of
  a question set out in an application will affect assessments or
  proposed assessments in respect of two or more persons who have been
  served with a copy of the application and who are named in an order of
  the Tax Court under this subsection, it may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if none of the persons so named has appealed from
    such an assessment, proceed to determine the question in any manner
    that it considers appropriate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if one or more of the persons so named has or have
    appealed, make any order joining a party or parties to that or those
    appeals that it considers appropriate and proceed to determine the
    question.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination final and conclusive

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), if a question set out in
  an application is determined by the Tax Court, the determination is
  final and conclusive for the purposes of any assessments of persons
  named by the Court under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a question set out in an application is determined
  by the Tax Court, the Minister or any of the persons who have been
  served with a copy of the application and who are named in an order of
  the Court under subsection (4) may, in accordance with the provisions
  of this Act, the [Tax Court of Canada Act](/eng/acts/T-2) or the
  [Federal Courts Act](/eng/acts/F-7), as they relate to appeals from or
  applications for judicial review of decisions of the Tax Court, appeal
  from the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties to appeal

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The parties who are bound by a determination are
  parties to any appeal from the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time during consideration not counted

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of making an assessment of the
  person, filing a notice of objection to an assessment or instituting
  an appeal from an assessment, the periods described in subsection (9)
  shall not be counted in the computation of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the four-year periods referred to in section 191;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which a notice of objection to an
    assessment may be filed under section 195; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period within which an appeal may be instituted
    under section 198.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded periods

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The period that is not to be counted in the
  computation of the periods described in paragraphs (8)(a) to (c) is
  the time between the day on which an application that is made under
  this section is served on a person under subsection (3) and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a person named in an order of the
    Tax Court under subsection (4), the day on which the determination
    becomes final and conclusive and not subject to any appeal; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any other person, the day on which
    the person is served with notice that the person has not been named
    in an order of the Tax Court under subsection (4).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, ss. 205, 410
:::

### [Records and Information]{.HTitleText2} {#h-181257 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping records --- general

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following persons shall keep all records that are necessary to
  determine whether they have complied with this Act:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} every licensee or registrant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} every person who is required under this Act to file
    a return;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} every person who makes an application for a refund
    that may be obtained under this Act; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} every person that transports a tobacco product,
    cannabis product or vaping product that is not stamped or
    non-duty-paid packaged alcohol.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping records --- tobacco growers and
  provincial tobacco marketing boards

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every tobacco grower and every body established under
  provincial law for the marketing of raw leaf tobacco grown in the
  province shall keep records that will enable the determination of the
  amount of raw leaf tobacco grown, received or disposed of by them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping records --- cannabis licensee

  [(2.01)]{.lawlabel} Every cannabis licensee shall keep records that
  will enable the determination of the amount of cannabis product
  produced, received, used, packaged, re-worked, sold or disposed of by
  the licensee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping records --- vaping product
  licensee

  [(2.02)]{.lawlabel} Every vaping product licensee shall keep records
  that will enable the determination of the amount of vaping product
  manufactured, received, used, packaged, re-worked, sold or disposed of
  by the licensee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping records --- tobacco
  manufacturing equipment

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who possesses tobacco manufacturing
  equipment (other than equipment that is designed for use by an
  individual manufacturing manufactured tobacco or cigars for their
  personal use as permitted under subsection 25(3) and that is not
  designed for commercial manufacturing) shall keep records that will
  enable the determination of the source, the type and the disposition
  of that equipment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Keeping records --- excise stamps

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who has been issued an excise stamp
  shall keep all records that are necessary to determine the receipt,
  retention, location, use or disposition of the stamp.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may specify information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may specify in writing the form a record
  is to take and any information that the record must contain.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Language and location of record

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise authorized by the Minister, a record
  shall be kept in Canada in English or French.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic records

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every person required under this Act to keep a record
  who does so electronically shall ensure that all equipment and
  software necessary to make the record intelligible are available
  during the retention period required for the record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inadequate records

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a person fails to keep adequate records for the
  purposes of this Act, the Minister may, in writing, require the person
  to keep any records that the Minister may specify and the person shall
  keep the records specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period for retention

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is required to keep records shall
  retain them until the expiry of six years after the end of the year to
  which they relate or for any other period that may be prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 206
- 2008, c. 28, s. 59
- 2010, c. 12, s. 41
- 2018, c. 12, s. 77

  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objection or appeal

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person who is required under this Act to keep records serves a notice
  of objection or is a party to an appeal or reference under this Act,
  the person shall retain every record that pertains to the
  subject-matter of the objection, appeal or reference until the
  objection, appeal or reference is finally disposed of.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Demand by Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that it is
  necessary for the administration or enforcement of this Act, the
  Minister may, by a demand served personally or by registered or
  certified mail, require any person required under this Act to keep
  records to retain those records for any period that is specified in
  the demand and the person shall comply with the demand.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permission for earlier disposal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who is required under this Act to keep
  records may dispose of them before the expiry of the period in respect
  of which they are required to be kept if written permission for their
  disposal is given by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Electronic funds transfer

**[[207.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207.1 .sectionLabel}** For greater
certainty, information obtained by the Minister under Part XV.1 of the
[Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3) may be used for the purposes of this
Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 20, s. 74
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to provide records or
information

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other provision of this
  Act, the Minister may, subject to subsection (2), for any purpose
  related to the administration or enforcement of a listed international
  agreement or of this Act, by a notice served or sent in accordance
  with subsection (1.1), require any person to provide the Minister,
  within any reasonable time that is stipulated in the notice, with

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any information or additional information,
    including a return under this Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} A notice referred to in subsection (1) may be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} served personally;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sent by registered or certified mail; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sent electronically to a
    [[bank]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[credit
    union]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in
    subsection 123(1) of the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15), that has
    provided written consent to receive notices under subsection (1)
    electronically.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unnamed persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall not impose on any person (in this
  section referred to as a "third party") a requirement to provide
  information or any record relating to one or more unnamed persons
  unless the Minister first obtains the authorization of a judge under
  subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judicial authorization

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A judge of the Federal Court may, on application by
  the Minister and subject to any conditions that the judge considers
  appropriate, authorize the Minister to impose on a third party a
  requirement relating to an unnamed person or more than one unnamed
  person (in this section referred to as the "group") if the judge is
  satisfied by information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person or group is ascertainable; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the requirement is made to verify compliance by the
    person or persons in the group with any duty or obligation under
    this Act.

- [(4) to (6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2013, c. 33, s. 53\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 22, s. 208
- 2007, c. 18, s. 116
- 2013, c. 33, s. 53

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1306619-1311675.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-208-20130626.html "Link to previous version of section 208"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance order

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  summary application by the Minister, a judge may, despite section 224,
  order a person to provide any access, assistance, information or
  record sought by the Minister under section 208 or 260 if the judge is
  satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person was required under section 208 or 260 to
    provide the access, assistance, information or record and did not do
    so; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of information or a record, the
    information or record is not protected from disclosure by
    solicitor-client privilege.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An application must not be heard before the end of
  five clear days from the time the notice of application is served on
  the person against whom the order is sought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Judge may impose conditions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The judge making an order may impose any conditions
  in respect of the order that the judge considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contempt of court

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a person fails or refuses to comply with an order,
  a judge may find the person in contempt of court and the person is
  subject to the processes and the punishments of the court to which the
  judge is appointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An order by a judge may be appealed to a court having
  appellate jurisdiction over decisions of the court to which the judge
  is appointed. An appeal does not suspend the execution of the order
  unless it is so ordered by a judge of the court to which the appeal is
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of solicitor-client privilege

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(b), an accounting
  record of a legal counsel and any invoice, voucher or cheque that
  relates to the record is deemed not to be protected from disclosure by
  solicitor-client privilege.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time period not to count

**[[209.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209.1 .sectionLabel}** The following
periods of time shall not be counted in the computation of the period of
time within which an assessment of a person may be made under section
188 or 189:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the person is served or sent a notice of a
  requirement under subsection 208(1), the period of time between the
  day on which an application for judicial review in respect of the
  requirement is made and the day on which the application is finally
  disposed of; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if an application is commenced by the Minister under
  subsection 209(1) to order the person to provide any access,
  assistance, information or record, the period of time between the day
  on which the person files a notice of appearance, or otherwise opposes
  the application, and the day on which the application is finally
  disposed of.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2018, c. 27, s. 64
- [2021, c. 23, s. 75](#1306624-1311677){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1306624-1311677.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-209.1-20181213.html "Link to previous version of section 209.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of [[foreign-based information or
record]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[foreign-based information or
  record]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any information or record that is
  available or located outside Canada and that may be relevant to the
  administration or enforcement of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to provide foreign-based
  information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other provision of this Act, the Minister
  may, by a notice served or sent in accordance with subsection (3.1),
  require a person resident in Canada or a non-resident person that
  carries on business in Canada to provide any foreign-based information
  or record.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The notice shall set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reasonable period of not less than 90 days for
    the provision of the information or record;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a description of the information or record being
    sought; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the consequences under subsection (8) to the person
    of the failure to provide the information or record being sought
    within the period set out in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} A notice referred to in subsection (2) may be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} served personally;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sent by registered or certified mail; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} sent electronically to a
    [[bank]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} or [[credit
    union]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as those terms are defined in
    subsection 123(1) of the [Excise Tax Act](/eng/acts/E-15), that has
    provided written consent to receive notices under subsection (2)
    electronically.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of foreign information
  requirement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a person is served or sent a notice of a
  requirement under subsection (2), the person may, within 90 days after
  the day on which the notice is served or sent, apply to a judge for a
  review of the requirement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers on review

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On hearing an application in respect of a
  requirement, a judge may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confirm the requirement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vary the requirement if satisfied that it is
    appropriate in the circumstances; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set aside the requirement if satisfied that it is
    unreasonable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement not unreasonable

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (5), a requirement to
  provide information or a record shall not be considered to be
  unreasonable because the information or record is under the control of
  or available to a non-resident person that is not controlled by the
  person on which the notice of the requirement is served, or to which
  that notice is sent, if that person is related to the non-resident
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time period not to count

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The period between the day on which an application
  for the review of a requirement is made and the day on which the
  application is finally disposed of shall not be counted in the
  computation of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the period set out in the notice of the
    requirement; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which an assessment may be made
    under section 188 or 189.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consequence of failure

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If a person fails to comply substantially with a
  notice served or sent under subsection (2) and the notice is not set
  aside under subsection (5), any court having jurisdiction in a civil
  proceeding relating to the administration or enforcement of this Act
  shall, on the motion of the Minister, prohibit the introduction by
  that person of any foreign-based information or record described in
  that notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions applicable to confidentiality
provisions

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  definitions in this subsection apply in this section.

  [[aboriginal government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aboriginal government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an aboriginal
      government as defined in subsection 2(1) of the
      [Federal-Provincial Fiscal Arrangements
      Act](/eng/acts/F-7.8). ([gouvernement autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[authorized person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[authorized person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      engaged or employed, or who was formerly engaged or employed, by
      or on behalf of Her Majesty to assist in carrying out the
      provisions of this Act. ([personne autorisée]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[business number]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[business number]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the number (other
      than a Social Insurance Number) used by the Minister to identify

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a licensee or a registrant for the purposes of
        this Act; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an applicant for a refund under this
        Act. ([numéro d'entreprise]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[confidential information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[confidential information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means information
      of any kind and in any form that relates to one or more persons
      and that is

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtained by or on behalf of the Minister for
        the purposes of this Act;

      - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} obtained by or on behalf of the Minister of
        Public Safety and Emergency Preparedness for the purposes of
        section 68; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prepared from information referred to in
        paragraph (a) or (a.1).

      It excludes information that does not directly or indirectly
      reveal the identity of the person to whom it relates and, for the
      purposes of applying subsections (3), (8) and (9) to a
      representative of a government entity that is not an official, it
      includes only the information referred to in paragraph
      (6)(j). ([renseignement confidentiel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[contact information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[contact information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a
      holder of a business number, means the name, address, telephone
      number, facsimile number and preferred language of communication
      of the holder, or similar information as specified by the Minister
      in respect of the holder, and includes such information in respect
      of one or more

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} trustees of the holder, if the holder is a
        trust;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} members of the holder, if the holder is a
        partnership;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} officers of the holder, if the holder is a
        corporation; or

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} officers or members of the holder, in any other
        case. ([coordonnées]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[corporate information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[corporate information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a
      holder of a business number that is a corporation, means the name
      (including the number assigned by the incorporating authority),
      date of incorporation, jurisdiction of incorporation and any
      information on the dissolution, reorganization, amalgamation,
      winding-up or revival of the corporation. ([renseignements
      d'entreprise]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[court of appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[court of appeal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      section 2 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([cour
      d'appel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[government entity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[government entity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a department or agency of the government of
        Canada or of a province;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a municipality;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an aboriginal government;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a corporation all of the shares (except
        directors' qualifying shares) of the capital stock of which are
        owned by one or more persons each of which is

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province,

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a municipality, or

        - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a corporation described in this paragraph;
          or

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} a board or commission, established by Her
        Majesty or Her Majesty in right of a province, that performs an
        administrative or regulatory function of government, or by a
        municipality, that performs an administrative or regulatory
        function of a municipality. ([entité
        gouvernementale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[municipality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[municipality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an incorporated city,
      town, village, metropolitan authority, township, district, county
      or rural municipality or other incorporated municipal body however
      designated. ([municipalité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[official]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[official]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is employed in
      the service of, who occupies a position of responsibility in the
      service of, or who is engaged by or on behalf of, Her Majesty or
      Her Majesty in right of a province, or a person who was formerly
      so employed, who formerly occupied such a position or who formerly
      was so engaged. ([fonctionnaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[registration information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[registration information]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a
      holder of a business number, means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any information pertaining to the legal form of
        the holder;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the type of activities carried on or proposed
        to be carried on by the holder;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} each date on which

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the business number was issued to the holder,

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the holder began activities,

        - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the holder ceased or resumed activities, or

        - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the business number assigned to the holder
          was changed; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the reasons for the cessation, resumption or
        change referred to in subparagraph (c)(iii) or
        (iv). ([renseignements relatifs à
        l'inscription]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[representative]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} of a government entity
      means a person who is employed in the service of, who occupies a
      position of responsibility in the service of, or who is engaged by
      or on behalf of, a government entity, and includes, for the
      purposes of subsections (2), (3), (8) and (9), a person who was
      formerly so employed, who formerly occupied such a position or who
      formerly was so engaged. ([représentant]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as authorized under this section, no official
  or other representative of a government entity shall knowingly

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide, or allow to be provided, to any person any
    confidential information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} allow any person to have access to any confidential
    information; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} use any confidential information other than in the
    course of the administration or enforcement of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidential information evidence not
  compellable

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament or other law, no
  official or other representative of a government entity shall be
  required, in connection with any legal proceedings, to give or produce
  evidence relating to any confidential information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Communications if proceedings have been
  commenced

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) and (3) do not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} criminal proceedings, either by indictment or on
    summary conviction, that have been commenced by the laying of an
    information or the preferring of an indictment, under an Act of
    Parliament; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any legal proceedings relating to the
    administration or enforcement of this Act, the [Canada Pension
    Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), the [Employment Insurance
    Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), the Unemployment Insurance Act or any other
    Act of Parliament or law of a province that provides for the
    imposition or collection of a tax or duty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authorized provision of information

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may provide appropriate persons with any
  confidential information that may reasonably be regarded as necessary
  solely for a purpose relating to the life, health or safety of an
  individual or to the environment in Canada or any other country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure of personal information

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An official may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide any confidential information to any person
    that may reasonably be regarded as necessary for the purpose of the
    administration or enforcement of this Act, solely for that purpose;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide a person with confidential information that
    can reasonably be regarded as necessary for the purposes of
    determining any liability or obligation of the person or any refund
    or other payment to which the person is or may become entitled under
    this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide, allow to be provided, or allow inspection
    of or access to any confidential information to or by any person, or
    any person within a class of persons, that the Minister may
    authorize, subject to any conditions that the Minister may specify;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} provide any person who is legally entitled under an
    Act of Parliament to confidential information with, or access to,
    that information, solely for the purposes for which the person is
    entitled to the information;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information, or allow the
    inspection of or access to confidential information, as the case may
    be, under, and solely for the purpose of,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 33.1(a) of the [Old Age Security
      Act](/eng/acts/O-9),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an order made under subsection 462.48(3) of the
      [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an order made under the [Mutual Legal
      Assistance in Criminal Matters Act](/eng/acts/M-13.6) to gather or
      send information, for the purposes of an investigation or
      prosecution relating to an act or omission that, if it had
      occurred in Canada, would constitute an offence for which an order
      could be obtained under subsection 462.48(3) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in response to a request made pursuant to

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} an administrative arrangement entered into
        under section 6 of the [Mutual Legal Assistance in Criminal
        Matters Act](/eng/acts/M-13.6), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a bilateral agreement for mutual legal
        assistance in criminal matters to which Canada is a party;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the Department of Finance
      solely for the purpose of the formulation or evaluation of fiscal
      policy,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purpose of the
      initial implementation of a fiscal policy or for the purposes of
      the administration or enforcement of the [Canada Pension
      Plan](/eng/acts/C-8), the [Employment Insurance
      Act](/eng/acts/E-5.6), the Unemployment Insurance Act or an Act of
      Parliament that provides for the imposition or collection of a tax
      or duty,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purposes of the
      administration or enforcement of a law of a province that provides
      for the imposition or collection of a tax or duty,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the government of a province
      solely for the purpose of the formulation or evaluation of fiscal
      policy,

    - [(iv.1)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purpose of the
      administration or enforcement of the [Canada Education Savings
      Act](/eng/acts/C-3.6) or a [[designated provincial
      program]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 146.1(1)
      of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),

    - [(iv.2)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purpose of the
      administration or enforcement of the [Canada Disability Savings
      Act](/eng/acts/C-3.5) or a [[designated provincial
      program]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection 146.4(1)
      of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} to an official of a department or agency of the
      Government of Canada or of a province as to the name, address,
      telephone number, occupation, size or type of business of a
      person, solely for the purpose of enabling the department or
      agency to obtain statistical data for research and analysis,

    - [(v.1)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the Department of Employment
      and Social Development solely for the purpose of the
      administration or enforcement of a program established under the
      authority of the [Department of Employment and Social Development
      Act](/eng/acts/H-5.7) in respect of children who are deceased or
      missing as a result of an offence, or a probable offence, under
      the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purpose of setting
      off, against any sum of money that may be due or payable by Her
      Majesty, a debt due to

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of a province on account
        of taxes payable to the province, if an agreement exists between
        Canada and the province under which Canada is authorized to
        collect taxes on behalf of the province,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purposes of
      section 7.1 of the [Federal-Provincial Fiscal Arrangements
      Act](/eng/acts/F-7.8),

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the Financial Transactions
      and Reports Analysis Centre of Canada solely for the purpose of
      enabling the Centre to evaluate the usefulness of information
      provided by the Centre to the Canada Revenue Agency under the
      [Proceeds of Crime (Money Laundering) and Terrorist Financing
      Act](/eng/acts/P-24.501),

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the Agency solely for the
      purpose of the collection of amounts owing to Her Majesty in right
      of Canada or of a province under the [Government Employees
      Compensation Act](/eng/acts/G-5), the [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2), the [Merchant Seamen Compensation
      Act](/eng/acts/M-6), the [Canada Student Loans
      Act](/eng/acts/S-23), the [Canada Student Financial Assistance
      Act](/eng/acts/S-22.7), the [Postal Services Continuation Act,
      1997](/eng/acts/P-15.8), the [Wage Earner Protection Program
      Act](/eng/acts/W-0.8), the [Apprentice Loans
      Act](/eng/acts/A-11.9) or a law of a province governing the
      granting of financial assistance to students at the post-secondary
      school level,

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the administration or
      enforcement of the [Tobacco and Vaping Products
      Act](/eng/acts/T-11.5) or the [Cannabis Act](/eng/acts/C-24.5),

    - [(xi)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the Agency solely for the
      purpose of the collection of amounts owing to Her Majesty in right
      of Canada under the Canada Emergency Business Account program
      established by Export Development Canada in accordance with an
      authorization made under subsection 23(1) of the [Export
      Development Act](/eng/acts/E-20),

    - [(xii)]{.lawlabel} to an official of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the Agency solely for the purposes of the
        administration or enforcement of the [Dental Benefit
        Act](/eng/acts/D-1.55), or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the Department of Health solely for the
        purposes of the formulation or evaluation of policy for that
        Act,

    - [(xii.1)]{.lawlabel} to an official of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the Department of Employment and Social
        Development, the Department of Health or the Department of
        Public Works and Government Services, solely for the purpose of
        the administration or enforcement of the Canadian Dental Care
        Plan established under the authority of the [Department of
        Health Act](/eng/acts/H-3.2) in respect of dental service for
        individuals, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the Department of Health solely for the purpose
        of the formulation or evaluation of policy for that plan,

    - [(xiii)]{.lawlabel} to an official of the Agency solely for the
      purposes of the administration or enforcement of the [Rental
      Housing Benefit Act](/eng/acts/R-5.44),

    - [(xiv)]{.lawlabel} to a person who is employed in the service of,
      who occupies a position of responsibility in the service of, or
      who is engaged by or on behalf of, the Canada Mortgage and Housing
      Corporation, solely for the purposes of the formulation or
      evaluation of policy for the [Rental Housing Benefit
      Act](/eng/acts/R-5.44), or

    - [(xv)]{.lawlabel} to an official solely for the purposes of a
      security review under subsection 83.032(10) or section 83.034 of
      the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), if the information can
      reasonably be considered to be relevant to the security review;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information solely for the
    purposes of sections 23 to 25 of the [Financial Administration
    Act](/eng/acts/F-11);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} use confidential information to compile information
    in a form that does not directly or indirectly reveal the identity
    of the person to whom the information relates;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} use, or provide any person with, confidential
    information solely for a purpose relating to the supervision,
    evaluation or discipline of an authorized person by Her Majesty in
    respect of a period during which the authorized person was employed
    by or engaged by or on behalf of Her Majesty to assist in the
    administration or enforcement of this Act, to the extent that the
    information is relevant for that purpose;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} use confidential information relating to a person
    to provide that person with information;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (6.1), provide to a
    representative of a government entity the business number of, the
    name of (including any trade name or other name used by), and any
    contact information, corporate information and registration
    information in respect of, the holder of a business number, if the
    information is provided solely for the purpose of the administration
    or enforcement of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an Act of Parliament or of a legislature of a
      province, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a by-law of a municipality or a law of an
      aboriginal government;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information to a police
    officer (within the meaning assigned by subsection 462.48(17) of the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)) solely for the purpose of
    investigating whether an offence has been committed under the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), or the laying of an information or
    the preferring of an indictment, if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the information can reasonably be regarded as
      being relevant for the purpose of ascertaining the circumstances
      in which an offence under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) may
      have been committed, or the identity of the person or persons who
      may have committed an offence, with respect to an official, or
      with respect to any person related to that official,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the official was or is engaged in the
      administration or enforcement of this Act, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the offence can reasonably be considered to be
      related to that administration or enforcement;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information, or allow the
    inspection of or access to confidential information, as the case may
    be, solely for the purposes of a provision contained in a [[tax
    treaty]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} (as defined in subsection 248(1) of
    the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3)) or in a listed international
    agreement;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information to any person
    solely for the purpose of enabling the Chief Statistician, within
    the meaning assigned by section 2 of the [Statistics
    Act](/eng/acts/S-19), to provide to a statistical agency of a
    province data concerning business activities carried on in the
    province, if the information is used by the statistical agency
    solely for research and analysis and the statistical agency is
    authorized under the law of the province to collect the same or
    similar information on its own behalf in respect of such activities;
    or

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} provide confidential information to a person who
    has --- under a program administered by the Canada Revenue Agency to
    obtain information relating to tax non-compliance --- entered into a
    contract to provide information to the Canada Revenue Agency, to the
    extent necessary to inform the person of any amount they may be
    entitled to under the contract and of the status of their claim
    under the contract.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions on information sharing

  [(6.1)]{.lawlabel} No information may be provided to a representative
  of a government entity under paragraph (6)(j) in connection with a
  program, activity or service provided or undertaken by the government
  entity unless the government entity uses the business number as an
  identifier in connection with the program, activity or service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public disclosure

  [(6.2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, in connection with a program,
  activity or service provided or undertaken by the Minister, make
  available to the public the business number of, and the name of
  (including any trade name or other name used by), the holder of a
  business number.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public disclosure by representative of
  government entity

  [(6.3)]{.lawlabel} A representative of a government entity may, in
  connection with a program, activity or service provided or undertaken
  by the government entity, make available to the public the business
  number of, and the name of (including any trade name or other name
  used by), the holder of a business number, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a representative of the government entity was
    provided with that information pursuant to paragraph (6)(j); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the government entity uses the business number as
    an identifier in connection with the program, activity or service.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serious offences

  [(6.4)]{.lawlabel} An official may provide to a law enforcement
  officer of an appropriate police organization

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confidential information, if the official has
    reasonable grounds to believe that the information will afford
    evidence of an act or omission in or outside of Canada that, if
    committed in Canada, would be

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an offence under any of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} section 3 of the [Corruption of Foreign Public
        Officials Act](/eng/acts/C-45.2),

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} sections 119 to 121, 123 to 125 and 426 of the
        [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} section 465 of the [Criminal
        Code](/eng/acts/C-46) as it relates to an offence described in
        clause (B), and

      - [(D)]{.lawlabel} sections 144, 264, 271, 279, 279.02, 281 and
        333.1, paragraphs 334(a) and 348(1)(e) and sections 349, 435 and
        462.31 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a terrorism offence or a criminal organization
      offence, as those terms are defined in section 2 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), for which the maximum term of imprisonment
      is 10 years or more, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} an offence

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} that is punishable by minimum term of
        imprisonment,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} for which the maximum term of imprisonment is
        14 years or life, or

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} for which the maximum term of imprisonment is
        10 years and that

        - [(I)]{.lawlabel} resulted in bodily harm,

        - [(II)]{.lawlabel} involved the import, export, trafficking or
          production of drugs, or

        - [(III)]{.lawlabel} involved the use of a weapon; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information setting out the reasonable grounds
    referred to in paragraph (a), to the extent that any such grounds
    rely on information referred to in that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Threats to security

  [(6.5)]{.lawlabel} An official may provide to the head of a recipient
  Government of Canada institution listed in Schedule 3 to the Security
  of Canada Information Disclosure Act, or to an official designated for
  the purposes of that Act by the head of that recipient institution,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} confidential information, if there are reasonable
    grounds to suspect that the information would be relevant to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an investigation of whether the activity of any
      person may constitute threats to the security of Canada, as
      defined in section 2 of the [Canadian Security Intelligence
      Service Act](/eng/acts/C-23), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an investigation of whether any of the following
      offences may have been committed:

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} a terrorism offence as defined in section 2 of
        the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} an offence under section 462.31 of the
        [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), if that investigation is
        related to a terrorism offence as defined in section 2 of that
        Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information setting out the reasonable grounds
    referred to in paragraph (a), to the extent that any such grounds
    rely on information referred to in that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures to prevent unauthorized use or
  disclosure

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The person presiding at a legal proceeding relating
  to the supervision, evaluation or discipline of an authorized person
  may order any measures that are necessary to ensure that confidential
  information is not used or provided to any person for any purpose not
  relating to that proceeding, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} holding a hearing [in camera]{.otherLang
    lang="la"};

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} banning the publication of the information;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} concealing the identity of the person to whom the
    information relates; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} sealing the records of the proceeding.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disclosure to person or on consent

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An official or other representative of a government
  entity may provide confidential information relating to a person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to that person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the consent of that person, to any other
    person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal from order or direction

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} An order or direction that is made in the course of
  or in connection with any legal proceedings and that requires an
  official or other representative of a government entity to give or
  produce evidence relating to any confidential information may, by
  notice served on all interested parties, be appealed immediately by
  the Minister or by the person against whom it is made to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the court of appeal of the province in which it is
    made, in the case of an order or direction made by a court or other
    tribunal established under the laws of the province, whether that
    court or tribunal is exercising a jurisdiction conferred by the laws
    of Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Federal Court of Appeal, in the case of an
    order or direction made by a court or other tribunal established
    under the laws of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The court to which an appeal is taken may allow the
  appeal and quash the order or direction appealed from or may dismiss
  the appeal, and the rules of practice and procedure from time to time
  governing appeals to the courts shall apply, with any modifications
  that the circumstances require, in respect of the appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} An appeal shall stay the operation of the order or
  direction appealed from until judgment is pronounced.
